## DAR: Darling Ingredients (52.5%, edge +5.0)
**Thesis:** DAR's fundamentals and estimate revisions are strong and momentum/quality are already priced into the elevated base rate, but the stock carries binary earnings risk within the 4-week window that could swing either way. Net edge is modestly positive given upgrade momentum and reasonable relative valuation.
**Key risk:** Earnings on 2026-10-21 could trigger a large adverse move given options pricing a 14.2% swing, well above historical norms, and commodity volatility could compress margins quickly.
**Exit if:** Exit if Q3 earnings (reported 2026-10-21) show operating margin contraction below 10% or management flags a negative shift in Fuel Ingredients/DGD contribution versus the $366.8M reported in Q2.
**Evidence:** D1, D7, D6, D5, D23
**Adjustments (sonnet):** +4 Sharp forward EPS revisions (+43.8% 90d, +6% 30d, 5 up/0 down) exceed base rate's backward-looking earnings reaction component, signaling continued analyst conviction (D7); +2 Strong positive news flow (5 good/0 bad, score +0.56) and fresh analyst PT raise to $88 add incremental sentiment support (D12, D14); +2 Favorable valuation vs peers (op-earnings yield percentile 77, P/S percentile only 64) suggests room despite momentum run-up (D6, D8); -3 Earnings on 10/21 falls within the 4-week window with unusually large implied move (14.2% vs 5.8% historical avg), adding binary risk not captured in base rate momentum/quality groups (D6, D42); -1 High commodity-price sensitivity flagged as red flag could amplify downside if oil/ag prices move against the Fuel Ingredients tailwind (D23, D41)
**Judges:** sonnet 51.5%; gpt_sol 50.5%; gemini_pro 55.5%; grok (shadow) 47.5%; kimi (shadow) n/a

## HLIT: Harmonic Inc. (52.3%, edge +3.0)
**Thesis:** HLIT's successful divestiture of its Video business and transition to a pure-play broadband provider has led to raised guidance and strong gross margin expansion. While extreme customer concentration and weak free cash flow remain risks, the positive news momentum and fresh strategic focus provide a slight edge for near-term outperformance.

## IESC: IES Holdings (48.3%, edge +2.7)
**Thesis:** Starting from the 45.6% base rate, exceptional backlog visibility modestly improves IESC’s four-week odds. Residential margin pressure, labor constraints, and a historically elevated P/S keep the forecast below 50%.
**Key risk:** Labor shortages prevent IESC from converting record backlog into profitable revenue.
**Exit if:** The next quarterly filing reports year-over-year backlog growth of 0% or less.
**Evidence:** D1, D22, D24, D23, D36, D38, D6
**Adjustments (gpt_sol):** +6 Record backlog and remaining performance obligations, including 116.3% year-over-year backlog growth, provide unusually strong near-term demand visibility. (D22, D24, D35); +2 The latest quarterly operating margin rose to 14.4%, supported by rapid growth across several nonresidential segments. (D23, D21); -2 Residential demand and pricing pressure are compressing that segment’s margins. (D19, D36); -2 Persistent skilled-labor constraints could limit execution against the backlog. (D20, D38); -2 P/S is at the 95th percentile of its own five-year history, creating a specific disappointment risk beyond the base rate’s general value classification. (D6)

## DELL: Dell Technologies (51.5%, edge +2.7)
**Thesis:** Dell's earnings estimate momentum and bullish analyst catalysts are real incremental positives, but they are roughly offset by verified filing-based margin pressure and working-capital strain tied to AI-server mix shift. The stock's already-extreme run and low quality score temper further outperformance, keeping the probability close to base rate.
**Key risk:** Non-linear AI server demand timing could cause a revenue/margin miss that reverses recent momentum sharply given the stock's elevated volatility (83% annualized).
**Exit if:** Gross margin rate falls below 17% in the next quarterly report, confirming the AI-mix/memory-inflation pressure flagged in filings.
**Evidence:** D1, D7, D21, D25, D32, D17

## SCSC: ScanSource, Inc. (48.7%, edge +1.3)

## ANF: Abercrombie & Fitch (45.1%, edge -0.7)
**Thesis:** While ANF has experienced substantial upward estimate revisions, underlying fundamentals reveal declining unit volumes and flat comparable sales masked by a one-time tariff refund. As this margin benefit fades in Q3, the stock is vulnerable to underperformance despite its recent momentum.
**Key risk:** Strong momentum and continued analyst upgrades could squeeze the elevated short interest, driving near-term outperformance.
**Exit if:** Short interest rises above 20% of float
**Evidence:** D5, D7, D21, D43
**Adjustments (gemini_pro):** +4 Analyst EPS estimates for the current fiscal year have surged 26.3% over the last 30 days with 5 upward revisions and no downward revisions. (D7); -5 A non-recurring $100M tariff refund significantly boosted Q2 margins but will drop to just $14M in Q3, exposing underlying SG&A deleverage and low-single-digit unit volume declines. (D21, D24, D41, D42, D43, D44); -2 Elevated short interest of 17.2% indicates significant market skepticism and downside risk against the stock's recent momentum. (D6)
**Judges:** sonnet 50.8%; gpt_sol 41.8%; gemini_pro 42.8%; grok (shadow) 43.8%; kimi (shadow) n/a

## REGN: Regeneron Pharmaceuticals (47.7%, edge -0.7)
**Thesis:** Strong revisions and Dupixent growth support REGN, but EYLEA erosion and filing-level setbacks leave a slight edge to the bear case. The dossier conflicts on the earnings date, so no directional credit is given for an earnings catalyst within four weeks.
**Key risk:** Continued combined EYLEA franchise erosion offsets Dupixent growth.
**Exit if:** Q3 results report combined EYLEA HD and EYLEA sales at or above the prior-year quarter.
**Evidence:** D1, D5, D7, D21, D30, D13, D47
**Adjustments (gpt_sol):** +2 Recent analyst revisions are unusually one-sided: 23 up and none down in 30 days. (D7); +1 Dupixent's 38% sales growth supports the near-term business outlook. (D20, D18); +1 Recent news flow is positive, though much of it reiterates known product trends. (D11, D15); -2 The combined EYLEA franchise contracted 21%, presenting a material offset to Dupixent growth. (D21, D25); -1 Net income declined despite revenue growth. (D19, D43); -1 The EYLEA HD pre-filled syringe timeline slipped, and two development programs were discontinued. (D30); -1 The Medicaid pricing agreement creates uncertainty about future U.S. net realization. (D24, D46)

## HPQ: HP Inc. (46.5%, edge -2.3)
**Thesis:** While HPQ screens well on value and has seen upward EPS revisions, its fundamental trajectory is concerning due to steep volume declines in PCs and sustained margin pressure from component inflation. **Key risk:** Continued aggressive ASP hikes fully offsetting volume drops without destroying further demand, or an unexpected drop in memory component costs.
